## Findings

- [high] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/DeviceZimScan.kt:292 — `cursor.extras` (`Cursor#getExtras`) is API 29+, but minSdk is 24 (build.gradle.kts:10) and the file itself guards 29/30/33-only APIs elsewhere (:119, :124, :128, :180); on Android 7-9 a granted-folder scan throws `NoSuchMethodError` (an `Error`, not caught by the `Exception` handlers at :248 and KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:919) on the plugin worker thread and kills the process. Fix: guard the `extras` read with `Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= 29`.
- [high] src-tauri/src/imports.rs:686 — the verifier publishes with `state.entries.get_mut(&receipt).unwrap()` before the `Verifying` check on the next line, while `unretire_acquisition` (imports.rs:337) *removes* a `Cancelled` entry for the acquisition: retire (352) → refused deletion → unretire (337) removes the entry → the worker's final `unwrap()` panics while the `Shared::state` guard is alive, poisoning that mutex so every later `enqueue`/`status`/`ready`/`retry`/`shutdown` `.lock().unwrap()` panics and the integrity worker is gone for the process lifetime — imports and verification then fail until restart. Trigger: the local user deletes/forgets a download whose import is still verifying and the deletion is refused. Fix: `let Some(entry) = state.entries.get_mut(&receipt) else { continue; };` (the shape already used at 642-651). {seen in 3/3 answers}
- [high] src-tauri/src/lib.rs:146 — `parse_options` calls `std::process::exit(0)` for `--help`/`--version`, and that parser also runs inside the *already running* instance (`forward_launch`:382 on Unix, the single-instance closure:867 on Windows/macOS); a second launch `kiwix-workbench --version` therefore terminates the running app from inside the process, skipping `Service::shutdown`, session persistence and the clean lock/profile handoff. Fix: handle `--help`/`--version` only before the instance handoff, and return an error instead of exiting when parsing a forwarded launch.
- [high] src-tauri/src/store.rs:1142 — `state_from_value` returns `normalized || !warnings.is_empty()`, and every caller persists on that flag (`open_profile`:1887, `load_mobile_state`:1655-1658) with no backup of the previous file, so any record `decode_records` skipped is written out of `state.json` on the next launch: `Bookmark`/`Highlight`/`LegacyBookmarkBook` all use `deny_unknown_fields` (175/221/163), so a bookmark written by a *newer* build (or a malformed `books` value) is silently and irreversibly deleted by the older build — directly contradicting the documented "skipped … never persisted back over the original" (1272-1273). Fix: do not treat warnings as normalizing; keep skipped records on disk until the user acts. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [high] src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:1319 — `interrupt_network_workers` calls `engine.stop().await` but never replaces the `OnceCell`, unlike `suspend` (953-956, "Foreground resumption lazily creates a distinct fresh session"); `engine()` (1173) keeps returning the stopped session, so after any `internet_denied()` retirement (1246-1253) or `"MOBILE_NETWORK_BLOCKED"` result (1260-1268) every later add/unpause targets a dead session, the retiring worker's `engine.delete` (1294) fails, and the permanent `retirement_failures` entry blocks resume with "Restart Kiwix, then resume" (1216-1217). Reachable on mobile whenever the transfer policy flips while foregrounded. Fix: swap in a fresh `OnceCell` the way `suspend` does and clear the matching failure entries.
- [medium] docs/windows-agent-tools/assemble-sdk.py:33 — `if out.exists(): shutil.rmtree(out)` deletes an operator-supplied path (`sys.argv[5]`) with no sanity, containment or confirmation check, so a mistyped or swapped argument (repo root, the libzim prefix it is about to read, home directory) is destroyed before any work starts. Fix: refuse unless the target looks like a previous SDK directory (contains `kiwix-sdk.json`) or require `--force`.
- [medium] package-lock.json:2136 — rollup 4.66.0's `optionalDependencies` in the lockfile contain `@napi-rs/lzma-linux-x64-gnu@1.5.1` (entry at :803, `os: ["linux"]`, `cpu: ["x64"]`, `engines: ^22.20 || ^24.12 || >=25`) alongside 26 `@rollup/rollup-*` packages and nothing else in the tree depends on it, so `npm ci` on a linux-x64 runner installs a native package unrelated to the build graph (verified in the lockfile; rollup declares no such dependency) — regenerate the lockfile from a trusted registry and confirm the diff before the next release. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [medium] scripts/android-nearby.py:330 — the keystore and key passwords are passed to `apksigner` on the command line (`--ks-pass pass:…`, `--key-pass pass:…`, with literal argparse defaults at :1186-1187), so both are readable from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and process accounting by any local process for the duration of signing. Fix: `--ks-pass env:VAR`/`file:` or stdin.
- [medium] scripts/generate-android-evaluation.py:279 — `suggestions = list(suggestion.getResults(0, 32))` holds python-libzim `Suggestion` objects, not path strings, yet `page["path"] not in suggestion_paths` compares a `str` against them (always true) and the objects are also serialized into `titleResults`; the same shape is at scripts/stage-streetzim-evaluation.py:355 — the title-suggestion assertion can never pass and the harness reports a false failure (or fails to serialize). Fix: compare `suggestion.path` values and serialize the paths.
- [medium] scripts/iphone-build.py:224 — the ad-hoc signing path signs the app bundle before the nested framework/dylib loop and never re-signs the bundle afterwards, so the bundle's CodeResources no longer match the final nested binaries. Fix: sign nested code first, then the bundle.
- [medium] scripts/iphone-build.py:367 — `Build.sign` signs only the widget extension and the main bundle; nested `Frameworks/**/*.framework` and `*.dylib` are never signed, unlike the other two signing paths in the same file (:224), so a physical-device install of a build containing nested frameworks fails validation or ships unsigned binaries. Fix: sign nested frameworks/dylibs before the bundle.
- [medium] scripts/iphone-ui/PhoneWindowUI.swift:192 — `tap(_:)` is declared non-throwing yet its three rejection paths use `try? reject(...)` (:192, :201, :209) and then `return element`, so a non-hittable control yields a reported success for a tap that never happened; every `try tap(...)` call site continues past a no-op tap and a run can reach later checkpoints and pass without touching the control. Fix: declare `tap` `throws`, use `try reject(...)`, and remove the unreachable `return element`.
- [medium] scripts/make-update-manifest.mjs:297 — the manifest's `pub_date`/`notes` are taken from caller-supplied arguments with no validation of the date format, and the generated JSON is written without the same atomic replace the staging path uses, so an interrupted run leaves a partially written `update.json` that the updater will fetch. Fix: validate the date and write atomically.
- [medium] scripts/profile-android-activity-memory.py:229 — the sampler treats a missing `TOTAL PSS` row as zero and continues, so a truncated `dumpsys meminfo` capture is recorded as a real measurement instead of aborting the sample. Fix: reject samples whose expected rows are absent.
- [medium] scripts/video-format-survey.py:170 — `_range_get` accepts an HTTP 200 reply to a `Range:` request whenever `start == 0` and then returns `resp.read()` unbounded (:172), so a mirror that ignores Range makes the "8 KiB header scan" stream an entire archive (catalog entries reach tens of GiB) into memory. Fix: bound the read and treat an oversized 200 as a scan error.
- [medium] scripts/video-format-survey.py:615 — `ANALYSIS_JSON.write_text(...)` rewrites the analysis ledger with only the current sample's `results`, discarding `existing` records whose archives are not in today's sample (loaded at 598-603 but never merged back), so previously collected evidence silently disappears on a re-run after the catalog sample shifts. Fix: merge `existing` with the new results.
- [medium] src-tauri/native/notifications.mm:475 — `nativeNotifications.failure` is read outside `@synchronized(self)` in `kiwix_notifications_call` while `call:` assigns it under the lock (:256, :276), and `failure` is a nonatomic strong property: a `capability` query racing a failing `configure` can read a pointer being released. (`validBundle` is written once under `dispatch_once`, so only `failure` is affected.) Fix: read both inside `@synchronized (nativeNotifications)` or expose a locked accessor.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/AndroidCompletionNotifications.kt:36 — `JSONObject(it.readBytes()…)` is unguarded, and `receiveIntent` (called on the Activity-creation path, KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:1445) throws `JSONException` out of `read` for a truncated/foreign journal, producing a launch-time crash loop until app data is cleared; the iOS counterpart validates the same journal and fails closed (notifications.mm:155-170). Reachable after filesystem-level truncation of the AtomicFile (renames are atomic, so not from a normal kill). Fix: catch the parse failure in `read` and treat it as absent state.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/AndroidCompletionNotifications.kt:116 — `notices` has no size bound (acknowledged notices are retained forever as tombstones, :158) and every `configure`/`admitted`/`takeTap`/`acknowledgeTap`/`receiveIntent` re-parses and rewrites the whole JSON on the main thread via `ApplicationResources.call` → `onMain`, while the iOS counterpart caps at 2048 notices / 32 taps / 1 MiB (notifications.mm:20-22). Fix: evict the oldest notice and reject an oversized journal.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/DeviceZimScan.kt:65 — the token LRU is capped at 2048 entries while `scan` returns up to `maxEntries.coerceIn(1, 4096)` archives (:87) and inserts one token each (:91-92); when the shell asks for more than 2048 entries (KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:915 allows up to 4096) the tokens for the first half of the returned rows are evicted before the UI uses them and `open` throws `SCAN_RESULT_EXPIRED` (:111) — size the map from the same cap or clamp `maxEntries` to 2048.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/ExternalArchiveMove.kt:136 — `validate()` calls `snapshot(uri, sha256)`, which re-reads and re-hashes the whole document, and it is invoked at :110 (cohort pre-flight), :115, :121 (inside `withVerifiedProviderMoveCohort`, i.e. for every remaining part on each iteration) and :123 — up to O(N²) full-document reads for a multi-part move of multi-GB archives before any unlink. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:1297 — `viewerResult` ignores `result.resultCode` and cancels the live "open" op and deletes its export scratch whenever the activity result is delivered, while success is signalled separately by the `KiwixExportChoiceReceiver` broadcast; the two deliveries have no ordering guarantee, so if the activity result lands first a successful share is reported as cancelled and the file the target app is reading is removed. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:1616 — `receiveDocuments` adds `clip.getItemAt(i).uri` to a `LinkedHashSet<Uri>` and dereferences `it.scheme` (:1622) with no null check, and it runs unguarded from `load` (306), `onResume` (344) and `onNewIntent` (391); any app can send `ACTION_SEND` with type `application/octet-stream` (a declared filter) and a URI-less `ClipData.Item`, producing an NPE on the main thread and a crash. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/LocalHotspot.kt:106 — `quicState` calls `args.getString("lease")` and `currentQuicInterface()` with no try/catch, unlike `quicRoute` (:80-102): `org.json` `getString` throws `JSONException` when the key is absent, and `interfaces()`/`physicalNetworkNames()` (:47-58) can throw `SocketException` (interface disappearing mid-enumeration) or `SecurityException` from `ConnectivityManager`; these escape a status query, so whether the app's webview caller gets a rejected promise or the process dies depends on the plugin handler (not in this file) — wrap the body and answer `{"available": false}` on failure. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/LocalHotspot.kt:126 — `start()` performs unchecked-exception network enumeration (`interfaceIdentities()` → `NetworkInterface.getNetworkInterfaces()`, declared `throws SocketException`, line 42; `physicalNetworkNames()` → `ConnectivityManager.getAllNetworks()`/`getLinkProperties`, line 57) at lines 126-127, i.e. before `pending = invoke` (line 129) and outside the try that starts at line 134; `quicState()` (line 106) calls the same enumeration path with no try at all — a `SocketException`/`SecurityException` escapes the plugin main-handler call, so the caller's Invoke is never resolved or rejected (the JS promise hangs forever) or the process aborts — wrap lines 126-127 and the `currentQuicInterface()` call in `quicState` in try/catch and `invoke.reject(...)` / return `available:false`. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/NearbyBluetooth.kt:228 — the GATT service is created with plain `PERMISSION_WRITE`/`PERMISSION_READ` (lines 228-231) and the server accepts the first device that connects (line 290 cancels everyone else), so frames travel over an unencrypted, unbonded link and any nearby BLE device — no authentication required — can take the single connection slot and write arbitrary channel frames; whether injected frames are acted on is bounded only by the Rust-side protocol the file header says holds the authority (not inlined), and the slot can always be occupied to block legitimate transfers — require link encryption/bonding (`PERMISSION_*_ENCRYPTED`, `setEncryptionRequired(true)` on the CCCD) before accepting channel traffic.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/NearbyBluetooth.kt:536 — `scan()` commits `owner.scanning = enabled` before the fallible `startScan(...)` on line 537 (which throws `NEARBY_BLUETOOTH_UNAVAILABLE` when `manager.adapter` is null, e.g. Bluetooth just switched off) and never rolls the flag back, so after a failed enable the guard on line 534 makes every later `scan(enabled=true)` a silent success that starts nothing — user-visible "scanning but never finds peers" until the app restarts or a disable/enable cycle happens — set `owner.scanning` only after `startScan` returns, and require `owner.started` here (as `connect` does on line 369) so a pre-`begin` call cannot make `started(owner)` on line 221 resolve the start invoke early.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/NearbyBluetooth.kt:597 — `retire()` swallows `stopScan`/`stopAdvertising` exceptions (lines 597-604) and then computes `clean` from `owner.scanner == null && owner.advertiser == null` (lines 620-622); the very condition that triggers retirement — the `ACTION_STATE_CHANGED` receiver failing the owner when the adapter leaves `STATE_ON` (lines 168-169) — makes `BluetoothLeScanner.stopScan` throw `IllegalStateException("BT Adapter is not turned ON")`, so the refs stay set, `clean` is false, and the owner is retained in `owners[owner.role]`, blocking every future `reserve` with `NEARBY_BLUETOOTH_BUSY` (line 112) because the fail path has already reported a terminal "failed" and nothing retries — clear the scanner/advertiser refs and treat adapter-off as a clean teardown (or schedule a retry) instead of retaining.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/OwnedDocuments.kt:214 — the copy loop accumulates and writes every chunk with no total-size or entry-count bound, and `visit` (:154) only guards depth/cycles, so a user-selected document or a misbehaving provider can stream unbounded bytes and directory entries into `cacheDir` until device storage is exhausted. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/BackgroundAcquisition.swift:566 — `retire` re-polls `getAllTasks` every 50 ms with no deadline, so a task that never disappears loops forever, `completion()` is never called, the Rust reply never arrives and the key stays in `retiring`, permanently failing later `start` for that key with `background_task_still_stopping` (:404); `pauseAll` (541-557) has the same unbounded wait on the resume-data callbacks. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/DocumentStorage.swift:81 — `capture(url, openInPlace:)` registers the security-scoped original before delegating to the copy/staging path, so a failure later in capture leaves a scoped resource registered for a document that was never retained. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/DocumentStorage.swift:404 — the staged-copy identity check compares only size/mtime from the pre-copy `stat` against a post-copy `stat`, so a provider that rewrites content in place without changing size or mtime passes the check and a substituted document is imported as the user's selection. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/NearbyBluetooth.swift:117 — the delegate validates only the first request of a prepared-write batch and answers only that one (see also :281/:394), so later requests in the batch are neither validated nor answered and the peer's transfer stalls. Fix: validate and answer every request in the batch.
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/NearbyBluetooth.swift:261 — a finished session's `Registration` is left in `registrations` (same for a prepared registration at :281), so the registry grows per transfer and a later reconnect can match a stale registration. - [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/NearbyBluetooth.swift:281 — the peer-supplied advertisement name (`CBAdvertisementDataLocalNameKey`, or UTF-8 service data at 267) is placed into `fields["name"]` unfiltered while only the log copy is sanitized (283-285), so arbitrary Unicode including control characters and newlines from any nearby advertiser crosses the BLE→JS bridge — consequence depends on how the webview renders the value (assumption: the JS side does not re-sanitize), i.e. injected control text or markup in a peer row — apply the same `controlCharacters`/`newlines` filter and length cap to the value stored in `fields` before `event("peer", …)`. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/NearbyBluetooth.swift:394 — the peripheral role accepts any central that subscribes (`subscriber == nil` is the only gate) and forwards its writes to JS (422), and the 18-byte identity block is whatever the connecting device serves from its own `info` characteristic (read at 407-412), so a rogue nearby device can both push packets into the app and self-assert any `publicId` — unless the JS payload protocol authenticates the peer (not visible in this file, stated as an assumption), a nearby unauthenticated attacker can inject data and impersonate a peer — require an expected-peer binding (pairing code or signed handshake) before emitting `bytes`/`connected` for a connection. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/ReaderAudioSession.swift:91 — `interruption(.began)` clears `speechOwnsActivation` but leaves `speechActive == true`, so a later `setSpeechActive(true)` short-circuits at :56 and returns `true` without calling `setActive(true)`: after a call/Siri interruption read-aloud produces no audio while the bridge reports success, and `shutdown()`/release can no longer deactivate the session. Fix: reset `speechActive` on `.began`, or re-activate when `speechActive && !speechOwnsActivation`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/acquisition/finalize.rs:198 — the publish-rejection branch clears `publication` and `job.sha256` only in the in-memory `State` and returns `Err` without `inner.save`, although the record *with* the publication intent was already persisted at :184; after a restart the durable record still carries the recovery proof the comment (199-201) says must be withdrawn, so a byte-identical foreign entry at the destination can be adopted as this job's publication. Fix: persist the withdrawn record (or clear the proof before the first save).
- [medium] src-tauri/src/acquisition/http_meta.rs:196 — a Metalink `<file>` with no `<hash type="sha-256">` element is accepted and yields `sha256: None` (:241), so the only integrity value left is the length taken from the same attacker-influenced document; `verify_expected_digest` (finalize.rs:22-29) only compares when the row carries a digest, so the missing state resolves to the permissive outcome (assumption: the worker copies `Metalink.sha256` into the job row, which worker.rs — not inlined — would confirm; reachability: whoever controls the Metalink document or the mirror it names, medium) — require a SHA-256 hash before accepting a Metalink, or fail closed when it is absent.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/acquisition/manager.rs:464 — `delete_terminal_files` holds the manager-wide `state` mutex from :464 through `delete_owned_file`, which re-hashes the complete durable SHA-256 of the published archive (`torrent.rs:389-405` via `with_publication`) before unlinking, so deleting a multi-GB download blocks every other download command (status/progress/pause) for the duration of a full-file hash. Fix: snapshot the ownership record, drop the lock, then hash/unlink.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/acquisition/segments.rs:298 — the 206 check requires `header_range.end == range.end` and then `Content-Length == expected` (:309), while RFC 7233 explicitly allows a server to satisfy a range request with fewer bytes; a CDN that caps range responses makes every segment fatal instead of short, so the download never completes from that mirror. Fix: accept a shorter `Content-Range` and re-request the remainder.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/acquisition/segments.rs:492 — the coordinator only records a stop when its own `signal.changed()` arm fires, but workers also exit with `Ok(())` on their own when the signal is not `Run` (:183) or when `bandwidth_permit` yields `None` (:345), so a pause/cancel racing those exits leaves the transfer's stop reason unrecorded. Fix: derive the outcome from the signal value after the join, not only from the select arm.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/acquisition/transfer.rs:484 — the read length is taken verbatim from `permit.bytes` (`reader.read(&mut buffer[..permit.bytes])`) while `buffer` is fixed at `READ_BUDGET` (line 469), and nothing in this file bounds or rejects a zero grant; a grant larger than `READ_BUDGET` panics on the slice and aborts the transfer task, and a zero-byte grant makes `read` return `Ok(0)`, which line 487 treats as EOF, so an unknown-length body (`total` and `response_bytes` both `None`, lines 300–301) falls through to `break 'transfer` at 584 and is published as a complete archive — reachable by the local user downloading from a chunked-200 mirror, with silent truncated-archive publication as the outcome (assumption: `bandwidth_permit`'s `Permit::bytes` is defined in the parent module, which was not available, and is not proven to be in `1..=READ_BUDGET`) — clamp the read to `buffer.len()` and treat a zero-byte permit as "yield and re-request" (`continue`) rather than EOF.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/android_evaluation.rs:235 — the only caller check is `context.getPackageName() == "org.kiwix.workbench"`, but `context` and its `getFilesDir()` (:237) are supplied by the caller, so the "guard" is compared against attacker-chosen data and the evaluation root is caller-chosen; any in-process caller can pass a stub Context returning that package name and its own directory, making every assertion in `run` (manifest, recipe, expected sha256 at :154, expected metadata at :172) self-consistent against caller-supplied fixtures, i.e. a forgeable "passing" report, plus a JSON read / directory-create / tree-delete primitive rooted anywhere that caller can reach — take the files root from a trusted source (app-owned filesDir) rather than from the passed `JObject`, and reject any path that is not under the canonical app files directory. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/src/catalog.rs:353 — `fetch_thumbnail` issues an HTTPS GET to a feed-supplied URL and the shared redirect policy (catalog.rs:186-200) re-validates only the *scheme*, never the host, so a user-subscribed or compromised OPDS feed can make the app send requests to arbitrary public or private HTTPS hosts (blind SSRF; the response is only displayed). Fix: reject private/loopback hosts on the initial URL and on every redirect.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/catalog_index.rs:372 — `series_compatible` returns `true` whenever either `flavour` is `None` while missing/extra variant tags fail closed (:371), so an edition whose `_flavour` metadata is absent is declared compatible with every explicit flavour (maxi/mini/nopic) of the same series — the highest-stakes dimension of edition replacement resolves permissively. Fix: require both flavours present and equal.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/exports.rs:1280 — `Html::parse_fragment` runs an unbounded html5ever parse (no `Bounds`, no cancellation) while the sibling `parse_document` is `#[cfg(test)]` (:1147) and the module doc (:885) requires untrusted documents to use `parse_document_bounded`; an archive-supplied document with deep/unclosed markup pins a worker and cannot be cancelled. Fix: route this path through the bounded parser.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/lib.rs:492 — in the read-only branch `fs::canonicalize(&path)?` aborts the whole launch on the first unresolvable path, while the writable branch and the unregistered-archive case (500-503) record a per-path entry in `errors` and continue — one stale path in the saved session prevents a read-only profile from opening at all. Fix: record and continue, as the sibling branches do.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/library_files.rs:33 — `library_files` and `library_root` each define their own `static OPERATIONS: Mutex<()>` (library_root.rs:43, taken at 302/414/549), and both are compiled together and reachable from the command layer, so a delete/move/export in `library_files` is not serialized against `switch`/`relocate_selected`/`recover` copying or cleaning the same file set — partial states, spurious "archive changed" failures, or two staging trees over one archive. Fix: one crate-wide `pub(crate)` lock.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/library_root.rs:559 — `recover` returns `Err` for any journal failing `read_journal`/`validate_journal` (corrupt file, `version` bump :1198, `profile` mismatch :1207 after a profile move/restore), nothing removes or quarantines it, and `switch` (:311)/`relocate_selected` (:418) refuse while `journal_path` exists — one unvalidatable journal disables all storage switching until the user hand-deletes `library-root-v1/migration.json`. Fix: rename it to `migration.invalid.json` and continue with a warning.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/library_root.rs:964 — `prepare_target` decides "already adopted" with `marker.exists()` + `fs::read`, both of which follow symlinks, while the archive open gate `is_managed_path` rejects a marker whose `symlink_metadata` is not a regular file (241-254): a destination whose `.kiwix-library-root.json` is a symlink is adopted, the switch commits paths there and (with `remove_old`) deletes the originals, after which every relocated archive fails `is_managed_path` and cannot be opened. Requires an outside writer able to place the symlink (legacy shared storage on Android ≤ 10, local tooling on desktop). Fix: require a regular marker via `symlink_metadata`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/library_root.rs:1347 — `write_atomic` opens the predictable sibling `<path>.tmp` with `create(true).truncate(true)` and no `create_new`/no-follow, unlike the other writes in the module (`copy_descriptor`:1085-1088, `library_files`), so a pre-existing symlink at that name is followed and truncated. Requires write access to the destination directory (defense-in-depth). Fix: `create_new` + `O_NOFOLLOW` on a random name.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/import.rs:91 — `self.manifest.chunk_hashes[chunk as usize]` indexes directly with `chunk` from `entry.start_chunk_index..=entry.end_chunk_index`, while the adjacent accessor at :76 (`chunk_len(chunk)?`) treats an out-of-range index as recoverable and the inlined manifest validation does not bound those entries against `chunk_hashes.len()` — a hostile manifest panics the import worker instead of being rejected. Fix: bounds-check and fail the manifest.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/ios_discovery.rs:445 — a failed or timed-out resolve is never retried: `invalidate()` clears `source.resolve`/`source.peer` but leaves `present == [true; 2]`, and `resolve()` is only re-issued from the `Event::Watch` arm (:495), so after the 20 s expiry (436-438) the peer stays listed as present with no live route until the next browse event. Fix: clear `present` on invalidate and re-resolve on expiry.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/library.rs:219 — `ack()` reads the single completion byte with no deadline (only `cancel.cancelled()` races it), so a peer that keeps its QUIC connection alive (keep-alives defeat the 120 s idle timeout, network.rs:192) but never sends the byte holds its `serve` task, its peer slot and a listener connection indefinitely. Fix: bound the ACK read.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/library.rs:322 — the client-side reply receive (`wire::receive`) has no deadline, while the server wraps the identical receive in a 5 s timeout (:233-236), so a discovered host that accepts the connection and stays silent hangs `browse` (:334) and `open` (:402) until the user cancels. Fix: apply the same bound client-side.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/library_transport.rs:104-121 — on the native (Apple) transport `self.connection` is `None` (:152), so a trailing-read error that is neither a cancellation nor a "made no progress" timeout skips the close-reason check (:105) and `complete()` returns `Ok(())`, whereas the Quinn branch fails closed; a reset Network.framework stream during the completion handshake is reported as a verified completion (library.rs:345/394 treat `Ok` as success). Fix: require an explicit clean-close confirmation for the native transport.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/library_transport.rs:320-325 — when the listener is created from a supplied socket (`binding` is `None`) the locality gate degrades to "any private/link-local address" instead of the on-link check the self-bound path uses, and `policy_revision` stays `None` (:287-290) so `local_policy_lost` is permanently pending — a peer on a routed private subnet (VPN/corporate LAN) is admitted, and local-policy revocation never retires it. Exploitation still needs the 128-bit session id, so this is a defense-in-depth degradation. Fix: derive a `LocalBinding` from the supplied socket and refuse when it cannot be computed.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/mod.rs:96 — `next_batch` restarts its scan at index 0 on every scheduling round and must walk the whole verified prefix before collecting `PIPELINE_WINDOW` missing chunks (and the retire scan at 995-999 rescans each worker's full `available` vector); with up to 262 144 chunks the loop runs per received chunk, making seeding quadratic in chunk count. Fix: keep a cursor/bitmap instead of rescanning.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/mod.rs:1196 — `checkpoint` serializes the entire job map (up to 64 MiB) and performs the blocking `directory.atomic("jobs.json", …)` write/fsync inline on a tokio worker while holding the `std::sync::Mutex` on `State`; the identical call at :958 is deliberately wrapped in `spawn_blocking`. Fix: serialize under the lock, write outside it.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/network.rs:855 — the pin is turned into `protocol::auth_token` (protocol.rs:325-329: a single unsalted `SHA256(swarm_id || ":" || pin)`) and the client sends it to whichever peer completed the handshake, i.e. before the peer has proven anything beyond possession of the shared embedded certificate that every copy of the binary holds (lines 147-152, 164) — a peer that impersonates the seeder at the dialed address learns the token and can recover a short numeric pin offline (the module's own fixtures use `"1234"` / `"738291"`), and there is no throttling on the online path either: the first mismatched `Auth` returns `Err` (line 1491-1492), so each guess costs only one connection while `max_incoming(32)`/`MAX_CONNECTIONS` (lines 221, 1187) allow unlimited attempts; assumption: the caller supplies a low-entropy numeric pin (not visible in this file) — if pins are long random secrets this drops to info — derive the token with an HMAC over a longer shared secret or a slow KDF and rate-limit failed `Auth` attempts per source.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/network.rs:1536 — after serving one chunk the seeder resets its next-request deadline to `next_request_allowance(read.len)` (1573-1575: `chunk_len * 256 + 45` seconds), which is 194 days for a 64 KiB chunk, while the initial deadline (:1458) is only `IO_TIMEOUT` — a peer that reads one chunk then stalls holds the seeding slot indefinitely. Fix: cap the allowance.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/protocol.rs:325 — the swarm's only peer credential is `auth_token = SHA-256(swarm_id || ":" || pin)` with no salt, KDF or freshness, and `network.rs:855` sends it to whichever peer completed the handshake (i.e. before the peer proves anything beyond the shared app-embedded certificate); a peer holding the app binary can capture the token and brute-force a short numeric PIN offline in seconds. Fix: use a password KDF with the session's ephemeral key material and only send the token after the peer proves possession of the PIN-derived secret.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/localswarm/sources.rs:171 — `reconnect_allowed` decides "link loss vs policy/cancellation/validation verdict" by substring-matching six human-readable literals against error text produced by string formatting in network.rs, so rewording any producer message silently changes reconnection behaviour (a policy denial can be retried, a link loss abandoned). Fix: classify with typed errors.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/maps.rs:180 — `MapDecision::validate` re-checks catalog/proxy/destination settings and the installed book but never compares the persisted privacy-policy epoch (`generation`, :161), so a decision authorized before a private/read-only transition still validates afterwards and the map job proceeds across the cutover. Fix: compare `generation` in `validate`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/maps/manager.rs:203 — lock-order inversion between the manager state mutex and the store lock: `prepare` holds `store.read()` (:181) while taking the state mutex (:203), and `start_prepared` does the same (:235, `start(...)`), so a caller taking them in the opposite order deadlocks. Fix: take the state mutex before the store guard, or drop the store guard first.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/maps/manager.rs:918 — `clear_cache` holds the manager state mutex (:918, guard live to 931) across `fs::remove_dir_all(&self.inner.cache)`, and `delete_download` does the same across `reclaim_published_map` and up to four persists — a large cache blocks every map command. Fix: drop the lock around the filesystem work.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/maps/manager.rs:1547 — the `Err(_)` arm matches the *outer* `store.with_acquisition_destinations_available` error ("destination unavailable"), not a per-name publish failure, yet it clears the publication stamp, persists the job and continues — a destination-unavailable error is handled as a publish collision and the recovery proof is discarded. Fix: match the specific publish error.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/maps/partials.rs:182 — `buffer.len().min(remaining as usize)` truncates the `u64` byte counter on 32-bit targets (Android armv7/i686), so a file whose remaining size is an exact multiple of 2^32 yields `count == 0`, `read_exact` makes no progress and the hash loop spins forever. Fix: compute the count in `u64` and cap with `buffer.len() as u64`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/migration.rs:150 — `bookmark.path.split_once("://")` matches `://` anywhere in the path, not only a leading scheme, so an archive-local path carrying a URL in its query/fragment (`A/Test?source=https://example.org`, the form the module's own tests treat as valid) is rewritten as a URL and the bookmark is lost. Fix: only treat a leading `scheme://` as a URL.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile.rs:181 — `lifecycle_generation` advances `generation`, `desired_active`, `checkpoint_transition` and the background request before the spawned `mobile_transition` runs; a failed transition is only recorded as `activation_retry` (215-222) / propagated (:250), leaving the recorded generation ahead of the actual native state. Fix: commit the generation only after the transition succeeds.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile.rs:646 — with `main.create = false` the Android main window is only built inside the `start` closure (:719), after every fallible step (`Service::new`:694, `mobile_initialize`:703, activity handoff:708-710); both failure branches (685-687, 739-746) call `show_startup_failure`, whose loop only evals into `get_webview_window("main")` (:593) — a startup failure therefore shows no boot-guard panel at all on Android. Fix: create the window before the fallible setup, or build the failure panel into a window that exists.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile.rs:739 — on Android the main window is only created inside `start()` at 717-720, after `Service::new` (694), and the config sets `create = false` (646); both failure branches (678-688 and 739-746) then call `show_startup_failure`, whose loop only evals into `get_webview_window("main")` (593) — a startup failure therefore leaves no window at all, the boot-guard panel never renders, and the user gets a blank app with no way to reach `reset_window_state` — build the window before `Service::new` or render the panel through a window created on the failure path. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile.rs:774 — every `RunEvent::ExitRequested` is prevented unconditionally, but the only arm that finishes the Activity (:823-841) is gated on a managed `Arc<Service>` (:775); after a startup failure no Service is managed, so the boot-guard "Quit" action reports `{"ok": true}` while the process keeps running. Fix: exit when no Service is managed.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_policy.rs:454 — both retirement acknowledgements are gated on one shared `errors` vector, so an `interrupt_internet`/`suspend`/`cancel`/`retire_catalogs` error leaves `local_retirement_pending` set (:456 skipped) and a `server.shutdown()` error leaves `retirement_pending` set — each domain's failure permanently blocks the other's admission. Fix: gate each acknowledgement on its own result.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_rebase.rs:97 — `array_mut(receipts)` and `receipt.as_str().ok_or(...)` (100) are stricter than the Store's own decoder, which tolerates a non-array `consumedAcquisitions` and non-string entries by skipping them (store.rs:1098-1111); a state the Store accepts therefore fails relocation, and the error surfaces as a hard startup failure through `rebase_json` at store.rs:1647 (or, for a profile with no readingSync/privateSync/edition keys, as a quarantine of `state.json` and an empty library via store.rs:1661-1668). Reachability: local profile written by an older/foreign build. — Smallest safe fix: skip non-array/non-string receipt entries (`continue` with a log) instead of erroring, matching `state_from_value`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_rebase.rs:167 — `path()` rejects any value that is not absolute/normalized, and that error aborts the entire record (`rewrite`→`rebase_json`), while `Store::validate` only requires a book path to be nonempty and NUL-free and legacy relative paths are resolved elsewhere (store.rs:2288): one legacy relative bookmark/history path aborts the whole profile rewrite, leaving every other path stale. Fix: skip the single record with a notice.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_rebase.rs:380 — `private_directory(directory)?` aborts `initialize` for a capability directory that is group/world-accessible or owned by another uid (:327), and the same unconditional-abort shape applies to a symlinked/non-directory `downloads`/`maps`/`library-migrations-v1` entry (:241 via 522/524) — a directory-permission or stray-symlink condition makes the app fail to initialize instead of reporting it. Fix: treat it like the recorded-notice paths.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_rebase.rs:529 — `name.to_str().ok_or(...)?` and `Uuid::parse_str(id).map_err(err)?` propagate out of `initialize` for a single unexpected entry in `library-migrations-v1/`, even though the surrounding loop is written to log and `continue` for invalid names (:530-535) — a non-UTF-8 name or a stray `notes.json` (backup restore, adb, another tool) prevents startup. Fix: continue with a notice.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_rebase.rs:556 — an unreadable-but-present `pending.json` (oversized, symlink, hard link, identity change at 442-449) leaves `pending_record = None`, so `publish(..., previous=None)` takes the no-replace branch (:303) against a file that exists and the relocation aborts. Fix: fail closed with a notice instead of attempting a no-replace publish.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_rebase.rs:592 — the root marker (and `pending.json`:556) is committed even when a planned record failed, because `plan_record` (:608-611) only logs and notifies; once the marker records `root: current` the next startup computes `relocation = None` and the unrewritten records are never revisited. Fix: commit the marker only when every planned record succeeded.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_service.rs:393 — `mobile_drain_native_entries` takes the whole native queue (`takeAppEntries`:387) and then propagates with `?` per entry (393, 394, 408), so one malformed entry drops every later queued entry (deep link, PROCESS_TEXT selection, download action) instead of skipping it. Fix: skip and count bad entries.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_speech.rs:527 — the `text`/`lang`/`voice`/`rate` payload is validated only after `begin_start` has retired the previous session (:515/525) and flipped this registration to `Phase::Starting`; a missing field returns `Err` (:530) without `speech_cleanup`/`finish_start`, leaving a registration that blocks later starts. Fix: validate before `begin_start`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_speech.rs:530 — the start path mutates state first (`begin_start` at 515-524 sets `Phase::Starting`, bumps `latest_started` and consumes older sessions via `speech_cleanup` at 525) and then returns `Err` on the payload-building paths (527, 530, 534) without retiring the capability — the registration is left in `Starting` with a live token, so `mobile_speech_retained()` (277) keeps returning true, `mobile_speech_released` (643) skips reader retirement while backgrounded, and repeated failures exhaust the 8-slot budget into permanent `SPEECH_BUSY` — retire the capability on these error paths exactly as the `mobile_call` failure path at 555-558 does. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/src/mobile_speech.rs:646 — the one-shot `media_retained` flag is cleared with `swap(false, …)` before `retire_readers` runs, and every later failure in the block (649, 652, 655) is log-only — if `retire_readers` fails, retained reader media is never retired and the flag no longer allows a retry. Fix: clear the flag only after retirement succeeds.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/native_pdf/linux.rs:24 — the GTK printer API is hand-declared (`gtk_printer_get_backend` typed as returning `*mut GTypeInstance`, the callback as `unsafe extern "C" fn(*mut c_void, *mut c_void) -> i32`) and every `unsafe` block in the file (16, 17, 24-32, 35-43, 47, 118-134) lacks a `// SAFETY:` justification, so a GTK ABI mismatch would be silent UB in the print path. Fix: document the ABI contract or use a typed binding.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/nearby_public_link.rs:99 — `encode` serializes `TemporaryNetwork.passphrase` (:35-36) as plaintext JSON and `framing.rs:37-44` emits it with an 8-byte cleartext header and no encryption/MAC/AEAD, while the module doc (:2) classifies the passphrase as a secret — anyone who can read the link payload learns the hotspot credential. Fix: omit the passphrase or encrypt it to the receiver.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/nearby_route.rs:59 — `bind_socket` calls the synchronous `self.check()` before the `spawn_blocking` hop, and on mobile `check()` (:32-39) performs `native(...)` → `MobileExt::call_sync`, a blocking cross-language IPC round trip executed directly on a tokio worker. Fix: move the check inside `spawn_blocking`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/nearby_route.rs:90 — the "socket owns its confirmed local address" guard only runs `if let Some(address)` (:92), and a native reply that omits or renames `localAddress` yields `None`, so the on-link verification is silently skipped for exactly the reply shape it is meant to validate. Fix: treat a missing address as a failure.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/nearby_service.rs:122 — the joined-route loss check compares `payload.get("lease")` with `owner.join_lease.as_deref()`, so a `{"kind":"lost"}` payload with no `lease` compares `None == None` and matches every owner that has not joined, cancelling unrelated work. Fix: require an explicit matching lease.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/nearby_service.rs:190 — `nearby_stop_owner` awaits the native stop calls and `task.await` with no timeout while every caller holds `nearby.gate` (206/208, 303/30x), so a native bridge that never returns wedges all nearby commands. Fix: bound the stop with a timeout.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/notifications_service.rs:78 — the mobile foreground wait after the OS permission sheet is an unbounded `loop` that only checks `ensure_running`/`ensure_window_active`/generations and otherwise sleeps 25 ms, with no deadline or cancellation, so a permission request whose root never becomes foreground again never settles (40 wakeups/s on Android while the process lives). Fix: add a deadline and reject.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/pdf_export.rs:89 — `drain_pdf_jobs` creates `self.pdf_idle.notified()` but never calls `.enable()` before the emptiness check, while the producer signals with `notify_waiters()` (:60) which stores no permit for a not-yet-registered waiter; the last job can drop between the check and the await, leaving the drain loop waiting forever. Fix: `let mut wake = notified(); wake.as_mut().enable();`.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/pdf_export.rs:104 — read-only enforcement is asymmetric: `share`/`print` jobs call `self.writable()?`, but `save_pdf`/`mobile_save_pdf` are admitted with no `writable()`/read-only check anywhere in this file (the comment at 101–103 claims read-only blocks them "exactly like saving") — assuming the dispatch layer does not separately gate the save commands, a user in strict read-only/kiosk mode can still write archive bytes to disk via PDF export while share/print are correctly refused — apply the same `self.writable()?` guard to the save commands in `admit_pdf`. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [medium] src-tauri/src/pdf_export.rs:329 — both the cancellation branch (:329) and the 90 s timeout branch (:334) call `let _ = (&mut export).await;` after `cancelPdfExport`, awaiting the same mobile IPC future with no further bound, so the "timeout" only bounds the first wait and a native side that never settles hangs the export task indefinitely. Fix: bound the post-cancel wait and drop the future.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/private_sync.rs:84 — the Noise handshake and every subsequent frame read (`read_packet`:162, `receive_chunk`:126) run on the raw `TcpStream` with no read deadline or frame timeout, so a peer that connects and stalls (or trickles bytes) pins a tokio task and its buffers indefinitely. Fix: wrap the handshake and each frame read in a timeout.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/reading_data.rs:415 — `validate_local_counter` only rejects context entries *above* `sync.counter` and `valid_register` (:320) accepts any dot the sender lists in its own `context`, so a paired peer can mint an event with dot `{receiver replica_id, counter <= sync.counter}` in a register it does not own. Fix: validate the dot against the register's owning replica.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/service/dispatch.rs:1504 — `catalog_thumbnail` fetches a caller-supplied `url` and returns the response with none of the admission checks its siblings take (`catalog`/`catalog_facets` at 1471-1477 do `ensure_running`, `ensure_window_active`, `ensure_generation` and hold `privacy_gate`); the same unguarded shape is at 2319 (`map_catalog` → `maps::fetch_catalog(&url)`) and 2327 (`map_recipe_metadata` → `maps::fetch_recipe_metadata(&url, …)`), both with `url` straight from `args` — a window that has been retired by a privacy transition (or a reader-hosted page that can reach the IPC surface, which the origin checks at 2498/2529 imply is in the threat model) still drives outbound requests to an arbitrary URL and reads the body back; the consequence is bounded only if `fetch_thumbnail`/`fetch_catalog`/`fetch_recipe_metadata` re-validate the host themselves (not visible here) — take `ensure_running`/`ensure_window_active`/`ensure_generation` and reject URLs outside the configured catalog/map origins before the fetch.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/shared_library.rs:96 — `revision` resolves to `None` whenever the descriptor carries an explicit `route` or native services, which disables `transfer_policy::require_local_since` (:226) and makes `local_policy_denied()` (`pending`, 264-269) never fire for exactly those peers. Fix: compute a revision for routed/native descriptors too.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/shared_library.rs:109 — the per-receipt caps (10 000 books, 4 096-byte titles) are checked but nothing bounds the aggregate, and the parsed `Vec<SharedBook>` is cloned once for the response (:128) and moved into the `Receipt` (:135) while up to 64 receipts are retained for 300 s, so a hostile peer can pin hundreds of MiB per library browse. Fix: bound the aggregate bytes and the retained total.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/sharing.rs:836 — the PIN gate allows 5 attempts per rolling 30 s with no escalation, lockout or reset on success (`attempt`/`authenticates`, :834-856), and the PIN may be as short as 4 digits (:810) — an on-LAN attacker brute-forces a 4-digit PIN in roughly 17 hours at 10 attempts/min against the shared library. Fix: exponential backoff plus a longer lockout, and raise the minimum PIN length.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/spelling.rs:797 — the candidate query applies `LIMIT MAX_CANDIDATES + 1` with no `ORDER BY` and the loop (:810) turns "more rows than the cap" into `Err`, while the distance-1 filter (:817) runs afterwards — a dictionary with many equally-near candidates makes suggestions fail instead of returning a bounded set. Fix: order deterministically and filter before applying the cap.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/store.rs:1692 — `reset_saved_windows` opens `store.lock` with `write(true).create(true)` and rewrites `state.json` via `write_state_value` (:1709) without consulting the saved `readOnly` policy that `claim_profile_lock` (:1587) and `new_mobile` (:1932) honor — the startup-failure "reset layout" action creates a lock file in and replaces the state file of a strict read-only profile. Fix: honor `saved_read_only` here.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/store.rs:2302 — the acquisition reservation check tests `state.books`, `edition_transactions` and `edition_intents` but never `provider_books`, although `book()` (:2323), `has_books()` (:2329) and `has_book_at_path()` (:2342) chain that overlay in — a session-only provider archive's path is not seen as reserved, so an acquisition can publish over it. Fix: include `provider_books` in the check.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/store.rs:2477 — `set_runtime_mode` assigns `inner.runtime_read_only = read_only` unconditionally, so a caller that toggles private mode and passes `read_only: false` clears the strict read-only flag installed from the startup policy, and `provider_books` admitted under 2373 is never dropped when leaving private/read-only mode. Fix: latch read-only (`runtime_read_only |= read_only`) and clear the provider overlay on exit.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:395 — `share` holds the exclusive lifecycle `gate` from :395 through `spawn_blocking(retain_archive_files)` (409-421) and a 20 × 100 ms polling loop (497-503) while the share worker it spawned needs the same gate, so a slow retention scan makes sharing fail on the poll deadline. Fix: release the gate before waiting.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:713 — `finish_terminal` clears the seed intent (`seeding = false`, `paused = true`, status `completed`) before the fallible retirement (:731-742); when `retired.take()` yields `None` or the retirement errors, the durable record claims the seed stopped while the engine may still be serving. Fix: clear the intent only after retirement succeeds.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:1137 — `update` clones the whole `State`, serializes it and writes `jobs.json` synchronously while holding the `std::sync::Mutex<State>`, and it is called from async worker bodies (`update_record` at 1590/1755/1948), blocking a runtime worker on fsync per tick. Fix: snapshot under the lock and write outside it.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:1220 — `while tasks.try_join_next().is_some() {}` discards every `Err(JoinError)`, so a panicking worker never reaches the cleanup at 1269-1314: its `Runtime` entry and torrent handle stay registered and the failure is invisible. Fix: log and record join errors, and remove the runtime entry.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:1886 — `engine.pause(&handle).await` runs while the async `inner.gate` acquired at 1881 is still held (same at 2171 and `engine.delete(...).await` at 1294 under the gate at 1269), contradicting the file's own rule that no gate is held across torrent work. Fix: drop the gate before awaiting the engine.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/publication.rs:94 — the legacy branch pushes `final_path` into `recovered` on `record.job.completed_paths.contains(&final_path)` alone, without statting the destination and without running the `policy` closure, unlike the intent branch (:88-92), so a stale history entry adopts a foreign or missing file as this job's publication. Fix: stat and apply the policy on the legacy path too.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/torrent/publication.rs:123 — every ZIM is flattened to `record.directory.join(file_name())` (:122) and the intent lookup (:83-86) and `import_publication` (:21-23) match by basename only, so a torrent carrying `a/x.zim` and `b/x.zim` (the storage collision class the manifest validation refuses) maps both to one destination. Fix: reject basename collisions or preserve the relative path.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/transfer_policy.rs:151 — `physical_interface` is a fixed macOS-style denylist (`utun`, `bridge`, `awdl`, `llw`, `lo`, `gif`, `stf`, `anpi`, `pdp_ip`), so common Linux/Windows virtual adapters (`wg*`, `tun*`, `tap*`, `tailscale0`, `docker0`, `veth*`) are treated as physical on-link interfaces for the "local network only" checks. Fix: classify by interface flags/type rather than name.
- [medium] src-tauri/src/watch.rs:274 — the folder watcher's per-event handling re-reads and re-validates the settled file inline on the watcher task with no bound on the work per event burst, so a monitored directory that receives a large batch (or an archive still being written) delays every later event for the whole profile. Fix: bound/debounce the per-event work and move it off the watcher loop.
- [medium] src/api.ts:904 — the authenticated preview bearer token is copied from the URL fragment into `sessionStorage` and left there for the tab's lifetime, so any script running in the preview origin can read it and replay it against the local `/api` endpoint, which exposes file-writing commands (`save_text`, `save_bytes`, `save_mhtml`) — reachable by anyone using the non-native browser-preview flow (the operator-facing path at :1253-1264) — keep the token in a module-level variable instead of Web Storage and drop it on privacy transitions. {seen in 3/3 answers}
