- [low] src/storage-bridge.js:299 — freeze leases are held in a `Set`, so `updateFreeze(freeze, true)` is idempotent, while the resume branch at line 306 releases with no `documentId` check (unlike the drain branch at line 298) — if `freeze` names a shared lease/reason rather than a unique per-drain id, two concurrent drains collapse into one lease and the first resume unfreezes both, so writes proceed while the shell still believes the document is drained — reference-count the leases (Map of id→count) and require `message.documentId === documentId` on resume as the drain branch does. Assumption: the shell may reuse a `freeze` value across drains; only the drain/resume handlers are visible here. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/storage-bridge.js:306 — the `kiwix-storage-resume` branch omits the `message.documentId === documentId` check (298) and the freeze-length bound (299) that the drain branch enforces, so a resume the shell addresses to a different or already-retired document still clears this document's freeze lease, and an unbounded freeze string is accepted into the persisted set — writes can proceed while the shell believes storage is frozen for a drain, and `readFreezes()` then parses a payload of arbitrary size on every write — add the documentId and `typeof`/length validation to the resume branch {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/storage-bridge.js:352 — `key(index)` rebuilds the whole map from native storage on every call (`readLocal`, 64-74), and `length` (349) and `refresh` (131-134) rescan the same way, so the standard `for (i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i++) localStorage.key(i)` loop is O(n²) native `Storage` calls — archives holding many keys stall the main thread on every enumeration and on every write — snapshot `readLocal()` once per turn (or cache it and invalidate on write) instead of rescanning per accessor {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/storage-bridge.js:484 — the `Expires` attribute is honored with no upper bound while `Max-Age` is capped at 400 days (line 492), so `document.cookie = "a=1; Expires=Fri, 01 Jan 2100 00:00:00 GMT"` yields a durable `cookie-set` operation with a ~75-year expiry — archive JS can create cookies that outlive the intended retention window and the shell receives no cap to enforce — clamp `expires` to `Date.now() + 34560000 * 1000` as well. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/suspended-retry.ts:25 — `ForegroundQueue.wait()` has no removal/cancel path: a waiter for a command whose owner is gone stays in `waiters` forever and is resolved by the next `resume()`, which re-runs `attempt()` unless the caller happened to pass `stillRelevant` (optional at line 48) — a command issued by a closed tab is replayed on the next foreground transition, and abandoned waiters accumulate for the queue's lifetime — have `wait()` return a cancellable handle (or check relevance inside `resume`) so dead commands are dropped rather than replayed. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [low] src/suspended-retry.ts:80 — the first `gate.wait()` has no timeout, cancellation or abort path (unlike the retry loop, which is bounded by `MAX_FALLBACK_RETRIES`), so in exactly the missed-event case the header describes at lines 5-8 (WebView visible while the shell stays inactive, no native event, no fallback timer) the promise never settles and the caller awaiting `retrySuspended` hangs forever holding `attempt`/`stillRelevant` closures — accept an `AbortSignal`/timeout and reject (or throw the last `suspended`) so the caller can drop the command. Assumption: the injected `ForegroundGate` implementation has no internal timer; that file is not in this batch. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/system-text-scale.ts:14 — only `Number.isFinite(scale) && scale > 0` is validated; the value is written straight into `--system-text-scale` with no sanity bound, so a finite-but-absurd scale from the native bridge (e.g. `1e6`, or a near-zero `1e-6`) sets a CSS var that collapses or explodes all shell typography — clamp to a sane range (roughly 0.5–3.5, which still covers the accessibility categories the comment wants uncapped) and fall back to 1 outside it. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/tab-stack.ts:23 — with `background` true and `activeTab` null or referring to a tab no longer in `tabs`, `findIndex` returns -1 and `index` becomes 0, so the new tab is inserted at the front of the strip instead of "at the end, or right after the active tab" as documented (and as the non-background branch does) — the tab order visibly jumps and the active/undo ordering assumptions in App break — `const found = tabs.findIndex(...); const index = background && found >= 0 ? found + 1 : tabs.length;`. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/tab-stack.ts:24 — the tab-stack reducer mutates the input array before returning a new one, so a concurrent reader of the previous state observes the mutation. Fix: copy first.
- [low] src/tab-stack.ts:39 — `removeReopened` returns a fresh array (`[...closed]`) even when `id` is absent, so a functional `setState` over the closed stack always gets a new reference and re-renders the tab strip on every no-op reopen — return `closed` unchanged when `index < 0` (or the caller should compare identity). {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/types.ts:381 — `AcquisitionImport["state"]` already includes `"imported"`, so the trailing `| "imported"` is a no-op. Fix: remove it. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/types.ts:619 — `EditionIntent.status` includes `"needsAuthorization"` while :622 declares a separate `needsAuthorization: boolean`, so two sources describe one state and can disagree. Fix: keep one. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [low] src/types.ts:648 — `UpdateProgress["phase"]` is declared `"downloading" | "verifying" | "installing" | string`, and `| string` absorbs the union (same shape at :651 for `platform` and nearby), so the type provides no exhaustiveness anywhere it is used. Fix: drop `| string` or add a separate open field. {seen in 3/3 answers}
- [info] scripts/iphone-ui/project.yml:15 — `CODE_SIGN_STYLE: Automatic` combined with an explicit `CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY: Apple Development` and no `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM` — on a clean machine or CI without a preinstalled profile, signing of the runner bundle fails unless the host passes `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM`/identity overrides — declare `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM` or document the required override next to the setting. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [info] scripts/iphone.py:320 — `validate_prepared(selected, device, sha256(selected))` computes the expected digest from the same bytes microseconds before validating them, so the mismatch branch at 118 can never fire; the call reads as tamper protection but protects nothing (the meaningful re-validation is at 457-458/484) — drop the argument or record one digest after selection and reuse it for the later re-validations.
- [info] scripts/profile-android-memory.py:218 — `self.adb('get-serialno')` runs as `adb -s <args.serial> get-serialno`, which by construction echoes back the serial that was passed in, so the `'Physical serial mismatch'` guard can never fire and provides no evidence that the intended physical device is the one attached; the only real admission checks are the `ro.product.model` and `ro.kernel.qemu` probes (lines 220-223) — reachability: operator script; if device identity proof is intended, compare against a device-reported value that adb does not derive from `-s` (e.g. `getprop ro.serialno`). {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [info] scripts/profile-android-memory.py:613 — `pair['oursMinusOfficialKiB']['pssKiB'] / 1024` is the only metric read in `summarize()` without the `None` guard used one screen up at line 609, so a `None` total would raise `TypeError` after all measurement work has completed and turn a finished run into a rejected one with no summary; with the current metric set `pssKiB` is always an integer (`parse_meminfo` raises at line 88 if absent), so this is not reachable today, but it breaks if a nullable metric is ever listed first or `TOTAL PSS` handling changes — apply the same `any(v is None ...)` guard used at line 609. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [info] scripts/test-native-location.mm:31 — `+locationServicesEnabled` is stubbed to a constant `YES` for the whole run (restored only at line 150), so the location-services-disabled branch of the production code is unreachable in every test case; a regression in that refusal path (wrong code, request left pending, delegate not cleared) cannot be caught — add a second case that flips the stub to `NO` before `begin(...)` and asserts the request terminates with the expected non-success code. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [info] scripts/test-reader-frame-admission.mjs:26 — `item.name.getText(tree) === "MISS_MARKER"` is a placeholder that can never match, so the variable-statement arm of the declaration filter is dead and any module-level `const` the extracted helpers depend on is silently omitted from the transpiled snippet (identical placeholder at scripts/test-reader-live-export.mjs:372 and scripts/test-reader-video-capability.mjs:372) — replace the marker with the real declaration names, or drop the branch, so extraction gaps are explicit rather than latent ReferenceErrors. {seen in 1/3 answers}
- [info] src-tauri/Cargo.toml:35 — `tokio = { version = "1", features = ["full"] }` enables every runtime component (process, signal, fs, net, macros) instead of the used subset, increasing build time and binary size. Fix: list the features actually used.
- [info] src-tauri/native/print.mm:95 — `NSLog(@"Kiwix print: presenting %@ (%ld pages)", self.title, …)` writes the user's article title to the unified system log on every print, where it persists in `log show` output. Fix: log only the page count.
- [info] src-tauri/src/android_evaluation.rs:235-236 — the `getPackageName() == "org.kiwix.workbench"` check is not a trust boundary: the string is read from the very `JObject` the caller supplied, so any in-process caller passing an object whose `getPackageName` returns that literal passes; the real boundary is that the symbol is only loadable inside this app's process. Not exploitable as written — noted so it is not mistaken for an authorization control. Fix: none required; drop the check or document it as an assertion. {seen in 2/3 answers}
- [info] src-tauri/src/exports.rs:68 — the top-level `Content-Type` is built from `root.mime` via `media_type()` before `checked_mime()` ever runs on it (the only validation is at :83, inside the part loop); the code's own "invalid MIME header" guard is therefore applied after first use — not demonstrably injectable today because `is_html_mime()` (:58) must accept the same first `;`-segment, but any future loosening of that gate turns a crafted ZIM's entry MIME into CRLF/parameter injection into the exported file — smallest fix: call `checked_mime(&root.mime)?` before the preamble `write!` at :68.
- [info] src-tauri/src/localswarm/apple_transport_interop_tests.rs:46 — every test in this file (and the native regressions at apple_transport.rs:332/338/345) is `#[ignore]`d for requiring a physical LAN interface, so the FFI completion/cancel/ACK contract is only exercised by hand. Fix: add a loopback/native fake so CI covers it.
- [info] src-tauri/src/localswarm/tests.rs:194 — the persistence test re-derives the expected auth token with its own `Sha256::digest(format!("{sid}:{secret}"))` instead of calling `protocol::auth_token`, so a change to the derivation is mirrored rather than caught. Fix: call the product function.
- [info] src-tauri/src/map_chunk_cache.rs:430 — `MAX_ENTRY_BYTES` (16 MiB) exceeds the total `MAX_BYTES` budget (8 MiB), so the per-entry cap never binds (documentation-level inconsistency; no runtime effect beyond the budget). Fix: order the constants.
- [info] src-tauri/src/mobile_policy.rs:12 — `NativeNetworkState` derives `Default`, which yields `metered: false, cellular: false`, while the fail-closed initializer at :80 sets `metered: true`; a future caller starting from `Default` would be permissive. Fix: implement `Default` explicitly as the fail-closed value.
- [info] src-tauri/src/store.rs:1272 — (documentation) the `load_warnings` field comment promises skipped records are "never persisted back over the original", which the high `state_from_value` finding shows is not what the code does; the comment must be fixed with the behaviour.
- [info] src-tauri/src/torrent.rs:1012 — the `MOBILE_NETWORK_BLOCKED` + `!can_restore_record(record)` branch returns before the counter reset at :1020-1022, so a record whose racing user action left it terminal keeps its last non-zero `speed`/`upload_speed`/`eta_seconds`; reachability is narrow because `pause`/`cancel` (src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:556-558, 615-617) zero the counters themselves, but `finish_terminal` (src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:713-717) sets `paused`/`completed` without zeroing — hoist the three resets above the branch so every exit path clears them.
- [info] src-tauri/src/torrent.rs:1489 — `validate_terminal_publications` joins the never-validated `CleanupEntry.name` from saved JSON onto `record.directory` (`Path::join` discards the base for an absolute name and lets `..` escape it) with no `safe_relative` check; it fails closed today only because the delete path matches the entry by name against a `safe_relative`-validated `record.files` member and requires matching directory/file identity (src-tauri/src/torrent/manager.rs:800-813), so a locally tampered history is rejected downstream rather than at load — add `safe_relative(Path::new(&ownership.name))` when validating `published_cleanup`.
- [info] src-tauri/src/windows.rs:45 — every FFI call in this file is an `unsafe` block with no `// SAFETY:` note (lines 45, 52, 61, 78, 97, 108, 112, 114, 203, 208); the doc comments state intent but not the pointer/lifetime invariant being upheld (non-null `ns_window`, snapshot ownership/free pairing at 108–114, main-thread requirement of `with_webview` at 201) — a future edit that reorders the free at 114 or drops the null check at 109 compiles silently and becomes use-after-free/null-deref — add one SAFETY line per block naming the invariant.

## Refuted in triage

The sweep produced 1123 finding lines. Every one was re-read against the cited file and enough surrounding context (callers, guards, manifests, startup validation, documented intent). 65 distinct findings were refuted and are listed first; 123 further lines were the same site or the same root cause as a kept finding and are folded at the end. The remaining 897 are kept above, with severities adjusted where the demonstrated consequence was narrower or wider than the original rating.

- src-tauri/native/localswarm.mm:70 (was high) — the base64 literal is the identity's ANSI X9.63 EC private key (`0x04‖X‖Y‖K`, 97 bytes for P-256), which is the documented input format for `SecKeyCreateWithData` with `kSecAttrKeyClassPrivate`; the literal matches `identity.key.pem` byte-for-byte, and `identity_signs()` verifies the key against the pinned leaf at startup, so a bad blob fails closed instead of mis-verifying. The shared identity is documented as application gating, not peer authentication (docs/LOCALSWARM.md:241, :359).
- src-tauri/src/localswarm/network.rs:149 (info) — same documented shared app identity; the private key is public material by design, not a leaked credential.
- src-tauri/src/localswarm/apple_transport.rs:31 (info) — same as above (the `include_bytes!` PKCS#12 is the same pinned identity).
- src-tauri/native/localswarm.mm:447 — no Rust caller queries the route/interface or joins from inside a native completion (`completed` only sends on a oneshot), and joins run in `spawn_blocking`, so the described `dispatch_sync` self-deadlock has no reachable caller.
- src-tauri/native/localswarm.mm:688 — `accepted()` always calls `Connection::start` before handing the connection to Rust, so an accepted handle cannot be cancelled before `start_connection` installs the state handler.
- src-tauri/native/localswarm.mm:25 — the only writer of these globals is `identity()`, which is behind a Rust `LazyLock`, so the stores happen once before any reader.
- src-tauri/native/localswarm.mm:585 — the only caller (`RawConnection::drop`) cancels before releasing, and every queued block copies the `shared_ptr` before it returns, so the described use-after-free needs a caller that does not exist.
- src-tauri/native/localswarm.mm:566 — the Rust consumer treats any non-zero status as an error and ignores `length`, so reporting the byte count on error has no effect.
- src-tauri/native/location.mm:62 — `locationManagerDidChangeAuthorization` re-enters `authorizeOrStart` when the user answers, and both the code comment (:75-76) and permissions.rs:543-544 state that permission-wait time is deliberately excluded from the acquisition deadline.
- src-tauri/native/location.mm:149 — tokens come from a monotonic `NEXT_NATIVE_TOKEN` counter (permissions.rs:508-525) and are never reused.
- src-tauri/native/pdf.mm:143 — every pointer comes from `CString::new(...)` over validated UTF-8 (native_pdf/apple.rs:37-39), so neither NULL nor invalid UTF-8 is reachable.
- src-tauri/native/pdf.mm:152, src-tauri/native/print.mm:171, src-tauri/native/share.mm:62 — ids/tokens are per-call `AtomicU64` counters (apple.rs:35/52, share.rs:54), so duplicate registration cannot occur.
- src-tauri/native/webview_backdrop.mm:24 — the only caller is inside `WebviewWindow::with_webview`, which executes on the main thread (windows.rs:200-215).
- src-tauri/native/webview_backdrop.mm:29 — the only caller passes `f64::from(u8) / 255.0` (windows.rs:209-213), which is always finite and in 0…1.
- src-tauri/src/android_evaluation.rs:230, :235 (medium) and :235 (info) — the module is compiled only under `#[cfg(all(feature = "android-evaluation", target_os = "android", debug_assertions))]` with a `compile_error!` guard (lib.rs:90-94), so the JNI entry points are absent from release builds and the package-name comparison is a sanity assertion, not an authorization control.
- src-tauri/src/android_evaluation.rs:206-212 — `Archive::open_files` clones the descriptor and uses `metadata()` for size (archive.rs:347-380); it never reads through the caller's stream cursor, so the EOF offset is irrelevant.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixSpeechService.kt:20 — `SpeechPlayback.foreground` calls `ServiceCompat.startForeground` and `stopSelf()` itself on the false path (SpeechPlayback.kt:124-134), so the FGS contract is satisfied.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixExportChoiceReceiver.kt:10 (×3) — the plugin manifest declares the receiver `android:exported="false"` (android/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:43).
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/AppEntryPoints.kt:41 — `allowBackup="false"` plus full `sharedpref` exclusions in both backup rule files (gen/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:21-23, res/xml/backup_rules.xml, data_extraction_rules.xml).
- src-tauri/plugins/.../DeviceZimScan.kt:65 — the only caller passes `maxEntries: 512` (mobile_service.rs:858), so the 2048-entry token LRU cannot evict a returned row.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/ExternalArchiveMove.kt:128 (×3) — `validate()` returns false only when `providerMoveMayDelete` sees a missing document while `attempting` (ProviderMoveIdentity.kt:9-13), i.e. after a delete already succeeded but before it was recorded, so `deleted = true` is correct; the pre-flight loop's discarded boolean is intentional (it exists to raise `PROVIDER_MOVE_IDENTITY_CHANGED`).
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/ExternalArchiveMove.kt:83 (×3) — receipts come from the app's own `prepare` and a malformed one is a caller bug; the plugin's `reject` maps `IllegalArgumentException` to the generic error surface.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/ExternalArchiveMove.kt:129 — receipts are keyed by an unguessable UUID held only by the Rust caller, are tiny, and still require an exact identity+digest match before acting.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/DownloadNotifications.kt:166 (×3, one medium) — the "timing oracle" would have to be measured across a Binder/activity-launch boundary against a 122-bit token delivered inside an immutable `PendingIntent`; not a practical attack.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/DownloadNotifications.kt:139 — Rust always supplies every action and status label (mobile_service.rs:1842-1849), so the `getString` throw is unreachable.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/DownloadNotifications.kt:42 — `activeCount()` is only called from `builder()`, which runs inside `update()` under `synchronized(AppEntryPoints)`.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/DownloadNotifications.kt:40 — the security of the action token rests on the random nonce, which is the secret; MD5 is only a derivation, not a MAC.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:903 — `DeviceZimScan.mediaParts` catches `SecurityException` and reports "Shared-storage access was denied…" as an explicit limitation (DeviceZimScan.kt:167-169).
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:542 — `NearbyBluetooth.begin` and the join path re-check permission and reject with `NEARBY_PERMISSION_DENIED` (NearbyBluetooth.kt:154).
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:1227 — the export destination comes from `ACTION_CREATE_DOCUMENT` (:962), which always creates a new empty document.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:803 — the only caller passes `MAX_EXPORT` = 16 MiB (mobile_service.rs:1172) and Rust re-checks the decoded length (:1175-1188).
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:1675 — a small preference commit on the intent path; no ANR-scale consequence is demonstrated and the failure path is handled.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/android/src/main/java/org/kiwix/mobile/KiwixMobilePlugin.kt:1722 — the value is written only by this class with `JSONArray` serialization; corruption needs external tampering, where the app is already outside its trust boundary.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/src/android_pdf.rs:17 (×3) — ids are fresh `Uuid::new_v4()` values per render (native_pdf/android.rs:22), so duplicate registration cannot occur.
- src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/BackgroundAcquisition.swift:680 — `urlSessionDidFinishEvents` is delivered after all delegate messages for the cycle, and `finishCompletionIfReady` clears the flag when it releases the handler, so the latch is the intended handshake.
- src-tauri/src/edition_updates.rs:209 — the entry is re-derived from the native catalog index and compared against the retained intent (edition_service.rs:104-122); the shell only supplies ids.
- src-tauri/src/edition_service.rs:287 — `enqueue_inner` returns the existing status for a known receipt with the same paths (imports.rs:261-267), so re-enqueueing per poll is idempotent.
- src-tauri/src/notifications.rs:165 — every native permission path calls the callback exactly once (notifications.mm:489-519).
- src-tauri/src/permissions.rs:528 — `finish:` is idempotent and `kiwix_location_cancel` routes through it (location.mm:158-162), so the boxed sender is reclaimed exactly once.
- src-tauri/src/native_pdf/apple.rs:40 — `kiwix_pdf_cancel` routes through `finish:` (pdf.mm:170-172) and the print cancel path has a settle timer that guarantees `finish:` (print.mm:100-119).
- src-tauri/src/ios_background.rs:143-147 (×2) — the Swift bridge replies on every path; the only path that can fail to reply is the unbounded retire poll, which is kept as the BackgroundAcquisition.swift:566 finding (duplicate root cause).
- src-tauri/src/os_open.rs:36 (×2) — the only URL caller validates through `external_link_target` (service.rs:3239-3257: https/http/mailto, no credentials, explicit confirmation), and the other callers pass app-owned paths.
- src-tauri/src/search.rs:123 — `limit` is clamped to 1…200 and `offset` to `u32` at the dispatch layer (service/dispatch.rs:690-696) before `run` is called.
- src-tauri/src/update.rs:390 — `acceptance_override` compiles to `None` outside debug builds (`#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]`), so a release build ignores `KIWIX_UPDATER_ENDPOINT`/`KIWIX_UPDATER_PUBKEY`.
- src-tauri/src/store.rs:2717 (×2) — documented tradeoff: an unreadable policy opens writable so the profile can be quarantined and repaired, while the desktop twin fails closed because it has a recovery UI (comment at :2714-2716).
- src-tauri/src/store.rs:74 (×2) — `serde_json::to_value` cannot fail for these derive-`Serialize` structs (plain `String`/`u64`/`Option` fields, no non-string map keys or non-finite floats), so the `expect` has no reachable trigger.
- src-tauri/src/maps/manager.rs:1471 — `Recipe::validate` rejects separators, control characters, leading dots and over-long stems (maps/recipe.rs:252-270) before the filename is built.
- src-tauri/src/maps/manager.rs:1198 — `Recipe::validate` enforces `size > 16` (recipe.rs:259), so `size - 16` cannot underflow for a validated recipe.
- src-tauri/src/maps/manager.rs:1376 — `Source::validate` enforces `chunk != 0`, `chunk <= MAX_CHUNK` and `mask[1] <= size - mask[0]` (recipe.rs:191-215), so the divide and the slice bounds are validated upstream.
- src-tauri/src/maps/recipe.rs:142 — the default catalog index is `https://streetzim.web.app/build/recipes/index.json` (maps.rs:77), so `index.join("../")` yields `/build/` and `recipes/a/b.json` resolves to `/build/recipes/a/b.json`, exactly as the comment describes.
- src-tauri/src/mobile_service.rs:142, src-tauri/src/notifications_service.rs:39 (×2), src-tauri/src/pdf_export.rs:108 — `__windowLabel` is inserted server-side from `window.label()` (lib.rs:183), so a missing label cannot be supplied by a caller.
- src-tauri/src/search.rs:368 — a forged continuation token grants no authority; it only affects the forger's own pagination.
- src-tauri/src/sharing.rs:2505, src-tauri/src/native_pdf.rs:184, src-tauri/plugins/tauri-plugin-kiwix-mobile/ios/Sources/NativeSpeech.swift:125 — constant-time comparison of a token the caller already holds; no attacker-observable timing channel.
- src-tauri/src/acquisition/manager.rs:35 — the loader already rejects `jobs.len() > MAX_JOBS` (persistence.rs:296) so the size guard is dead code, and the documented policy for unreadable/invalid records is an empty claim set (acquisition.rs:744-790); genuine I/O failures return `Unavailable`.
- src-tauri/src/acquisition/manager.rs:281 — the flag only extends iOS background execution; running entries are removed by the worker that sets `finished`, so the window is bounded by teardown.
- src-tauri/src/acquisition/manager.rs:725 — the part path embeds the per-job UUID (`acquisition.rs:900`), and a restart of an existing job resumes the durable row rather than calling `create_new`.
- src-tauri/src/acquisition/manager.rs:731 — the early return leaks only a zero-length temporary on an ownership-journal failure.
- src-tauri/src/acquisition/finalize.rs:213 — the publication intent is persisted before the rename (:184), so the next start's durable finalize re-adopts the exact verified inode and completes the row.

- .github/workflows/build.yml:378 (was medium) — `stage-update-release.mjs --configure` explicitly merges the generated `.native-windows.json` bundle into the release overlay on Windows (`Object.assign(overlay.bundle, JSON.parse(await readFile('.native-windows.json')).bundle)`, stage-update-release.mjs:47-50), so the DLL resource map is not lost on the release path.
